79:T14c5,<emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p><code>let</code> and <code>const</code> declarations define variables that are scoped to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#running-execution-context"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#running-execution-context">running execution context</a></emu-xref>'s LexicalEnvironment. The variables are created when their containing <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records">Environment Record</a></emu-xref> is instantiated but may not be accessed in any way until the variable's <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LexicalBinding">LexicalBinding</a></emu-nt> is evaluated. A variable defined by a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LexicalBinding">LexicalBinding</a></emu-nt> with an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Initializer">Initializer</a></emu-nt> is assigned the value of its <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Initializer">Initializer</a></emu-nt>'s <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-AssignmentExpression">AssignmentExpression</a></emu-nt> when the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LexicalBinding">LexicalBinding</a></emu-nt> is evaluated, not when the variable is created. If a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LexicalBinding">LexicalBinding</a></emu-nt> in a <code>let</code> declaration does not have an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Initializer">Initializer</a></emu-nt> the variable is assigned the value <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> when the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LexicalBinding">LexicalBinding</a></emu-nt> is evaluated.</p>
      </div></emu-note>
      <h2>Syntax</h2>

7e:Tdd4,<h2>Syntax</h2>
    <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-ExpressionStatement" name="ExpressionStatement"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-ExpressionStatement">ExpressionStatement</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[Yield, Await]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-gann>[lookahead &notin; { <emu-t>{</emu-t>, <emu-t>function</emu-t>, <emu-t>async</emu-t> [no <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-LineTerminator">LineTerminator</a></emu-nt> here]</emu-gann> <emu-t>function</emu-t>, <emu-t>class</emu-t>, <emu-t>let</emu-t> <emu-t>[</emu-t> }] <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Expression">Expression</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[+In, ?Yield, ?Await]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-t>;</emu-t></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
    <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
      <p>An <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-ExpressionStatement">ExpressionStatement</a></emu-nt> cannot start with a U+007B (LEFT CURLY BRACKET) because that might make it ambiguous with a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-Block">Block</a></emu-nt>. An <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-ExpressionStatement">ExpressionStatement</a></emu-nt> cannot start with the <code>function</code> or <code>class</code> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#sec-keywords-and-reserved-words"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#sec-keywords-and-reserved-words">keywords</a></emu-xref> because that would make it ambiguous with a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes#prod-FunctionDeclaration">FunctionDeclaration</a></emu-nt>, a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes#prod-GeneratorDeclaration">GeneratorDeclaration</a></emu-nt>, or a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes#prod-ClassDeclaration">ClassDeclaration</a></emu-nt>. An <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-ExpressionStatement">ExpressionStatement</a></emu-nt> cannot start with <code>async function</code> because that would make it ambiguous with an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes#prod-AsyncFunctionDeclaration">AsyncFunctionDeclaration</a></emu-nt> or a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes#prod-AsyncGeneratorDeclaration">AsyncGeneratorDeclaration</a></emu-nt>. An <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-ExpressionStatement">ExpressionStatement</a></emu-nt> cannot start with the two token sequence <code>let [</code> because that would make it ambiguous with a <code>let</code> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LexicalDeclaration">LexicalDeclaration</a></emu-nt> whose first <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LexicalBinding">LexicalBinding</a></emu-nt> was an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-ArrayBindingPattern">ArrayBindingPattern</a></emu-nt>.</p>
    </div></emu-note>

    84:T90e,<p>The abstract operation LoopContinues takes arguments <var>completion</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Record</a></emu-xref>) and <var>labelSet</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of Strings) and returns a Boolean. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">If <var>completion</var> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">normal completion</a></emu-xref>, return <emu-val>true</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>completion</var> is not a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">continue completion</a></emu-xref>, return <emu-val>false</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>completion</var>.<var class="field">[[Target]]</var> is <emu-const>empty</emu-const>, return <emu-val>true</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>labelSet</var> contains <var>completion</var>.<var class="field">[[Target]]</var>, return <emu-val>true</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-val>false</emu-val>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>Within the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-Statement">Statement</a></emu-nt> part of an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-IterationStatement">IterationStatement</a></emu-nt> a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-ContinueStatement">ContinueStatement</a></emu-nt> may be used to begin a new iteration.</p>
        </div></emu-note>

8d:T85c,<h2>Syntax</h2>
    <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-ReturnStatement" name="ReturnStatement"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-ReturnStatement">ReturnStatement</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[Yield, Await]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-t>return</emu-t> <emu-t>;</emu-t></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-t>return</emu-t> <emu-gmod>[no <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-LineTerminator">LineTerminator</a></emu-nt> here]</emu-gmod> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Expression">Expression</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[+In, ?Yield, ?Await]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-t>;</emu-t></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
    <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
      <p>A <code>return</code> statement causes a function to cease execution and, in most cases, returns a value to the caller. If <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Expression">Expression</a></emu-nt> is omitted, the return value is <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>. Otherwise, the return value is the value of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Expression">Expression</a></emu-nt>. A <code>return</code> statement may not actually return a value to the caller depending on surrounding context. For example, in a <code>try</code> block, a <code>return</code> statement's <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Record</a></emu-xref> may be replaced with another <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">Completion Record</a></emu-xref> during evaluation of the <code>finally</code> block.</p>
    </div></emu-note>

8f:Ta48,<emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
      <p>Use of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/conformance#sec-conformance"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/conformance#sec-conformance">Legacy</a></emu-xref> <code>with</code> statement is discouraged in new ECMAScript code. Consider alternatives that are permitted in both <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#sec-strict-mode-code"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#sec-strict-mode-code">strict mode code</a></emu-xref> and <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#non-strict-code"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#non-strict-code">non-strict code</a></emu-xref>, such as <emu-xref href="#sec-destructuring-assignment"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#sec-destructuring-assignment">destructuring assignment</a></emu-xref>.</p>
    </div></emu-note>

    <h2>Syntax</h2>
    <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-WithStatement" name="WithStatement"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-WithStatement">WithStatement</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[Yield, Await, Return]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-t>with</emu-t> <emu-t>(</emu-t> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Expression">Expression</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[+In, ?Yield, ?Await]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-t>)</emu-t> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-Statement">Statement</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[?Yield, ?Await, ?Return]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
    <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
      <p>The <code>with</code> statement adds an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-object-environment-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-object-environment-records">Object Environment Record</a></emu-xref> for a computed object to the lexical environment of the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#running-execution-context"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#running-execution-context">running execution context</a></emu-xref>. It then executes a statement using this augmented lexical environment. Finally, it restores the original lexical environment.</p>
    </div></emu-note>

98:Ta1b,<h2>Syntax</h2>
    <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-LabelledStatement" name="LabelledStatement"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LabelledStatement">LabelledStatement</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[Yield, Await, Return]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-LabelIdentifier">LabelIdentifier</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[?Yield, ?Await]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-t>:</emu-t> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LabelledItem">LabelledItem</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[?Yield, ?Await, ?Return]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production>

<emu-production id="prod-LabelledItem" name="LabelledItem"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LabelledItem">LabelledItem</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[Yield, Await, Return]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-Statement">Statement</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[?Yield, ?Await, ?Return]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-functions-and-classes#prod-FunctionDeclaration">FunctionDeclaration</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[?Yield, ?Await, ~Default]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
    <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
      <p>A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-Statement">Statement</a></emu-nt> may be prefixed by a label. Labelled statements are only used in conjunction with labelled <code>break</code> and <code>continue</code> statements. ECMAScript has no <code>goto</code> statement. A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-Statement">Statement</a></emu-nt> can be part of a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LabelledStatement">LabelledStatement</a></emu-nt>, which itself can be part of a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-LabelledStatement">LabelledStatement</a></emu-nt>, and so on. The labels introduced this way are collectively referred to as the “current label set” when describing the semantics of individual statements.</p>
    </div></emu-note>

    <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
      <p>The <code>try</code> statement encloses a block of code in which an exceptional condition can occur, such as a runtime error or a <code>throw</code> statement. The <code>catch</code> clause provides the exception-handling code. When a catch clause catches an exception, its <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-CatchParameter">CatchParameter</a></emu-nt> is bound to that exception.</p>
    </div></emu-note>

          <ul normative-optional>

a7:T12b2,<emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>A <code>var</code> statement declares variables that are scoped to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#running-execution-context"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#running-execution-context">running execution context</a></emu-xref>'s VariableEnvironment. Var variables are created when their containing <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-environment-records">Environment Record</a></emu-xref> is instantiated and are initialized to <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> when created. Within the scope of any VariableEnvironment a common <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-BindingIdentifier">BindingIdentifier</a></emu-nt> may appear in more than one <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-VariableDeclaration">VariableDeclaration</a></emu-nt> but those declarations collectively define only one variable. A variable defined by a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-VariableDeclaration">VariableDeclaration</a></emu-nt> with an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Initializer">Initializer</a></emu-nt> is assigned the value of its <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Initializer">Initializer</a></emu-nt>'s <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-AssignmentExpression">AssignmentExpression</a></emu-nt> when the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-VariableDeclaration">VariableDeclaration</a></emu-nt> is executed, not when the variable is created.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>No matter how control leaves the <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-SwitchStatement">SwitchStatement</a></emu-nt> the LexicalEnvironment is always restored to its former state.</p>

          It is a Syntax Error if any element of the <emu-xref aoid="BoundNames"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/syntax-directed-operations#sec-static-semantics-boundnames">BoundNames</a></emu-xref> of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-CatchParameter">CatchParameter</a></emu-nt> also occurs in the <emu-xref aoid="LexicallyDeclaredNames"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/syntax-directed-operations#sec-static-semantics-lexicallydeclarednames">LexicallyDeclaredNames</a></emu-xref> of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-Block">Block</a></emu-nt>.
        </li>
        <li>
          It is a Syntax Error if any element of the <emu-xref aoid="BoundNames"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/syntax-directed-operations#sec-static-semantics-boundnames">BoundNames</a></emu-xref> of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-CatchParameter">CatchParameter</a></emu-nt> also occurs in the <emu-xref aoid="VarDeclaredNames"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/syntax-directed-operations#sec-static-semantics-vardeclarednames">VarDeclaredNames</a></emu-xref> of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-Block">Block</a></emu-nt><span normative-optional>, unless <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-CatchParameter">CatchParameter</a></emu-nt> is <emu-grammar><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#prod-CatchParameter">CatchParameter</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:</emu-geq> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-BindingIdentifier">BindingIdentifier</a></emu-nt></emu-grammar> and the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#host">host</a></emu-xref> is a web browser or <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#sec-additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#sec-additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers">otherwise supports</a></emu-xref> <emu-xref href="#sec-variablestatements-in-catch-blocks" title><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/additional-ecmascript-features-for-web-browsers#sec-variablestatements-in-catch-blocks">VariableStatements in Catch Blocks</a></emu-xref></span>.
        </li>
      </ul>

            If <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-LeftHandSideExpression">LeftHandSideExpression</a></emu-nt> is either an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-ObjectLiteral">ObjectLiteral</a></emu-nt> or an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-ArrayLiteral">ArrayLiteral</a></emu-nt>, <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-LeftHandSideExpression">LeftHandSideExpression</a></emu-nt> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#must-cover"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#must-cover">must cover</a></emu-xref> an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-AssignmentPattern">AssignmentPattern</a></emu-nt>.
          </li>
          <li>
            If <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-LeftHandSideExpression">LeftHandSideExpression</a></emu-nt> is neither an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-ObjectLiteral">ObjectLiteral</a></emu-nt> nor an <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-ArrayLiteral">ArrayLiteral</a></emu-nt>, it is a Syntax Error if the <emu-xref aoid="AssignmentTargetType"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/syntax-directed-operations#sec-static-semantics-assignmenttargettype">AssignmentTargetType</a></emu-xref> of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-LeftHandSideExpression">LeftHandSideExpression</a></emu-nt> is <emu-const>invalid</emu-const>.
          </li>
        </ul>

e3:T41d,<emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-BindingIdentifier" name="BindingIdentifier"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-BindingIdentifier">BindingIdentifier</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-Identifier">Identifier</a></emu-nt></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-t>yield</emu-t></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-t>await</emu-t></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>bindingId</var> be the <emu-xref aoid="StringValue"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#sec-static-semantics-stringvalue">StringValue</a></emu-xref> of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#prod-BindingIdentifier">BindingIdentifier</a></emu-nt>.</li><li class="exit">Return ? <emu-xref aoid="ResolveBinding"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/executable-code-and-execution-contexts#sec-resolvebinding">ResolveBinding</a></emu-xref>(<var>bindingId</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>
      c3:["$","emu-clause",null,{"id":"sec-for-in-and-for-of-statements-runtime-semantics-evaluation","children":[["$","h1",null,{"id":"14758-runtime-semantics-evaluation","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-bold x:mt-2 x:text-4xl","children":[[["$","span",null,{"className":"secnum","children":"14.7.5.8"}]," Runtime Semantics: Evaluation"],["$","$L71",null,{"id":"14758-runtime-semantics-evaluation"}]]}],["$","div",null,{"dangerouslySetInnerHTML":{"__html":"$e3"}}]]}]
e4:T2890,<p>The abstract operation EnumerateObjectProperties takes argument <var>obj</var> (an Object) and returns an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator object</a></emu-xref>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li class="exit">Return an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator object</a></emu-xref> whose <code>next</code> method iterates over all the String-valued keys of enumerable properties of <var>obj</var>. The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator object</a></emu-xref> is never directly accessible to ECMAScript code. The mechanics and order of enumerating the properties is not specified but must conform to the rules specified below.</li></ol></emu-alg>
        <p>The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator</a></emu-xref>'s <code>throw</code> and <code>return</code> methods are <emu-val>null</emu-val> and are never invoked. The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator</a></emu-xref>'s <code>next</code> method processes object properties to determine whether the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key">property key</a></emu-xref> should be returned as an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator</a></emu-xref> value. Returned <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key">property keys</a></emu-xref> do not include keys that are Symbols. Properties of the target object may be deleted during enumeration. A property that is deleted before it is processed by the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator</a></emu-xref>'s <code>next</code> method is ignored. If new properties are added to the target object during enumeration, the newly added properties are not guaranteed to be processed in the active enumeration. A <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-name"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-name">property name</a></emu-xref> will be returned by the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator</a></emu-xref>'s <code>next</code> method at most once in any enumeration.</p>
        <p>Enumerating the properties of the target object includes enumerating properties of its prototype, and the prototype of the prototype, and so on, recursively; but a property of a prototype is not processed if it has the same name as a property that has already been processed by the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator</a></emu-xref>'s <code>next</code> method. The values of <var class="field">[[Enumerable]]</var> attributes are not considered when determining if a property of a prototype object has already been processed. The enumerable <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-name"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-name">property names</a></emu-xref> of prototype objects must be obtained by invoking EnumerateObjectProperties passing the prototype object as the argument. EnumerateObjectProperties must obtain the own <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#property-key">property keys</a></emu-xref> of the target object by calling its <var class="field">[[OwnPropertyKeys]]</var> internal method. Property attributes of the target object must be obtained by calling its <var class="field">[[GetOwnProperty]]</var> internal method.</p>
        <p>In addition, if neither <var>obj</var> nor any object in its prototype chain is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#proxy-exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#proxy-exotic-object">Proxy exotic object</a></emu-xref>, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#typedarray"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#typedarray">TypedArray</a></emu-xref>, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#module-namespace-exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#module-namespace-exotic-object">module namespace exotic object</a></emu-xref>, or implementation provided <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic object</a></emu-xref>, then the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator</a></emu-xref> must behave as would the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/control-abstraction-objects#sec-iterator-interface">iterator</a></emu-xref> given by <emu-xref aoid="CreateForInIterator"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-createforiniterator">CreateForInIterator</a></emu-xref>(<var>obj</var>) until one of the following occurs:</p>
        <ul>
          <li>the value of the <var class="field">[[Prototype]]</var> internal slot of <var>obj</var> or an object in its prototype chain changes,</li>
          <li>a property is removed from <var>obj</var> or an object in its prototype chain,</li>
          <li>a property is added to an object in <var>obj</var>'s prototype chain, or</li>
          <li>the value of the <var class="field">[[Enumerable]]</var> attribute of a property of <var>obj</var> or an object in its prototype chain changes.</li>
        </ul>

        <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>ECMAScript implementations are not required to implement the algorithm in <emu-xref href="#sec-%foriniteratorprototype%.next"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-%foriniteratorprototype%.next">14.7.5.10.2.1</a></emu-xref> directly. They may choose any implementation whose behaviour will not deviate from that algorithm unless one of the constraints in the previous paragraph is violated.</p>
          <p>The following is an informative definition of an ECMAScript generator function that conforms to these rules:</p>
          <pre><code class="hljs javascript">
            <span class="hljs-keyword">function</span>* <span class="hljs-title class_">EnumerateObjectProperties</span>(obj) {
              <span class="hljs-keyword">const</span> visited = <span class="hljs-keyword">new</span> <span class="hljs-title class_">Set</span>();
              <span class="hljs-keyword">for</span> (<span class="hljs-keyword">const</span> key <span class="hljs-keyword">of</span> <span class="hljs-title class_">Reflect</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">ownKeys</span>(obj)) {
                <span class="hljs-keyword">if</span> (<span class="hljs-keyword">typeof</span> key === <span class="hljs-string">&quot;symbol&quot;</span>) <span class="hljs-keyword">continue</span>;
                <span class="hljs-keyword">const</span> desc = <span class="hljs-title class_">Reflect</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">getOwnPropertyDescriptor</span>(obj, key);
                <span class="hljs-keyword">if</span> (desc) {
                  visited.<span class="hljs-title function_">add</span>(key);
                  <span class="hljs-keyword">if</span> (desc.<span class="hljs-property">enumerable</span>) <span class="hljs-keyword">yield</span> key;
                }
              }
              <span class="hljs-keyword">const</span> proto = <span class="hljs-title class_">Reflect</span>.<span class="hljs-title function_">getPrototypeOf</span>(obj);
              <span class="hljs-keyword">if</span> (proto === <span class="hljs-literal">null</span>) <span class="hljs-keyword">return</span>;
              <span class="hljs-keyword">for</span> (<span class="hljs-keyword">const</span> protoKey <span class="hljs-keyword">of</span> <span class="hljs-title class_">EnumerateObjectProperties</span>(proto)) {
                <span class="hljs-keyword">if</span> (!visited.<span class="hljs-title function_">has</span>(protoKey)) <span class="hljs-keyword">yield</span> protoKey;
              }
            }
          </code></pre>
        </div></emu-note>
        <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
          The list of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic objects</a></emu-xref> for which implementations are not required to match <emu-xref aoid="CreateForInIterator"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-createforiniterator">CreateForInIterator</a></emu-xref> was chosen because implementations historically differed in behaviour for those cases, and agreed in all others.
        </div></emu-note>

e5:T871,<p>The abstract operation CreateForInIterator takes argument <var>obj</var> (an Object) and returns a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-for-in-iterator-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-for-in-iterator-objects">For-In Iterator</a></emu-xref>. It is used to create a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-for-in-iterator-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-for-in-iterator-objects">For-In Iterator object</a></emu-xref> which iterates over the own and inherited enumerable string properties of <var>obj</var> in a specific order. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
          <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>iterator</var> be <emu-xref aoid="OrdinaryObjectCreate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-ordinaryobjectcreate">OrdinaryObjectCreate</a></emu-xref>(<emu-intrinsic>%ForInIteratorPrototype%</emu-intrinsic>, « <var class="field">[[Object]]</var>, <var class="field">[[ObjectWasVisited]]</var>, <var class="field">[[VisitedKeys]]</var>, <var class="field">[[RemainingKeys]]</var> »).</li><li>Set <var>iterator</var>.<var class="field">[[Object]]</var> to <var>obj</var>.</li><li>Set <var>iterator</var>.<var class="field">[[ObjectWasVisited]]</var> to <emu-val>false</emu-val>.</li><li>Set <var>iterator</var>.<var class="field">[[VisitedKeys]]</var> to a new empty <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref>.</li><li>Set <var>iterator</var>.<var class="field">[[RemainingKeys]]</var> to a new empty <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref>.</li><li class="exit">Return <var>iterator</var>.</li></ol></emu-alg>

e8:Tef6,<emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>iterator</var> be the <emu-val>this</emu-val> value.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>iterator</var> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">is an Object</a></emu-xref>.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>iterator</var> has all of the internal slots of a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-for-in-iterator-objects"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-for-in-iterator-objects">For-In Iterator</a></emu-xref> instance (<emu-xref href="#sec-properties-of-for-in-iterator-instances"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-statements-and-declarations#sec-properties-of-for-in-iterator-instances">14.7.5.10.3</a></emu-xref>).</li><li>Let <var>obj</var> be <var>iterator</var>.<var class="field">[[Object]]</var>.</li><li>Repeat,<ol><li>If <var>iterator</var>.<var class="field">[[ObjectWasVisited]]</var> is <emu-val>false</emu-val>, then<ol><li>Let <var>keys</var> be ?
